{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001","id":"msg_vrtx_018hkdemCJrZEPEfB8qotfFP","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01GWAjMp8adXp8LsZNj7WfJ1","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/environment/skills/suricata-signatures-basics.md","content":"# Suricata Signature Development Basics\n\n## Overview\nSuricata is an open-source network threat detection engine that uses rule-based pattern matching to identify suspicious traffic. Signatures (rules) define patterns to match against network flows.\n\n## Basic Signature Structure\n\nA Suricata rule has the following format:\n```\naction protocol source-ip source-port direction destination-ip destination-port ( rule-options; )\n```\n\n### Action Types\n- `alert` - Log the traffic and raise an alert\n- `drop` - Block the traffic\n- `pass` - Ignore the traffic\n- `reject` - Block and send rejection\n- `rejectsrc` - Reject source\n- `rejectdst` - Reject destination\n\n### Protocol\n- `tcp` - TCP traffic\n- `udp` - UDP traffic\n- `http` - HTTP traffic (TCP on port 80)\n- `tls` - TLS/SSL traffic\n- `icmp` - ICMP traffic\n- `ip` - Any IP traffic\n\n### Direction\n- `->` - Unidirectional (source to destination)\n- `<>` - Bidirectional\n\n### Rule Options (Key for HTTP/Application Detection)\n\n#### Flow Control\n```\nflow: established, to_server\nflow: to_server\n```\n- `established` - Only match established connections\n- `to_server` - Match traffic going to server\n- `to_client` - Match traffic going to client\n\n#### HTTP Protocol Keywords\n\n**Method Detection:**\n```\nhttp.method; content:\"POST\";\n```\n\n**URI/Path Detection:**\n```\nhttp.uri; content:\"/telemetry/v2/report\"; exact;\n```\n- `exact` - Match exactly (no substrings)\n\n**Request Header Detection:**\n```\nhttp.header; content:\"X-TLM-Mode|3a| exfil\";\n```\n- `|3a|` is hex for `:` (header separator)\n- Headers are case-insensitive\n\n**Body Content Detection:**\n```\nhttp.client_body; content:\"blob=\"; fast_pattern;\n```\n- `http.client_body` - Matches POST/PUT request body\n- `fast_pattern` - Use this pattern for fast path matching (only one per rule)\n\n#### Content Modifiers\n- `nocase` - Case-insensitive match\n- `distance:N` - Bytes after previous match\n- `offset:N` - Bytes from start of buffer\n- `within:N` - Match within N bytes of previous match\n- `isdataat:N` - Assert data exists at offset N\n- `fast_pattern` - Use as primary pattern for performance\n- `bsize:N` - Match buffer size is N bytes\n\n#### Pattern Matching\n- `pcre` - Perl Compatible Regular Expressions\n  ```\n  pcre:\"/pattern/options\";\n  ```\n  Common PCRE options: `i` (case-insensitive), `m` (multiline), `s` (dotall)\n\n- `content` - Literal byte matching with hex encoding\n  ```\n  content:\"blob=\";\n  content:\"|3d|\"; (matches `=`)\n  ```\n\n#### Byte Testing\n```\nbyte_test: size<bytes>, operator, value, offset [,relative] [,string] [,dce]\n```\n\n#### String Extraction\n```\npcre:\"/(?<capture>pattern)/\";\n```\n\n## HTTP-Specific Keywords\n\n### POST Request Detection\n```\nhttp.method; content:\"POST\";\n```\n\n### Exact URI Matching\n```\nhttp.uri; content:\"/path\"; exact;\n```\n\n### Header Matching\n```\nhttp.header; content:\"Header-Name|3a| value\";\n```\n\n### Request Body Matching\n```\nhttp.client_body; content:\"key=value\";\n```\n\n### Body Size Constraints\n```\nhttp.client_body; isdataat:100;\n```\n\n## Example: Simple POST Detection\n\n```\nalert http $HOME_NET any -> $EXTERNAL_NET any (\n    msg:\"Simple POST detection\";\n    flow:established,to_server;\n    http.method;content:\"POST\";\n    http.uri;content:\"/api/endpoint\";exact;\n    sid:1000001;\n    rev:1;\n)\n```\n\n## Performance Considerations\n\n1. **Fast Pattern**: Always use `fast_pattern` on the most specific/least common pattern\n2. **Order Matters**: Put more specific patterns before general ones\n3. **Content Ordering**: Earlier content matches are used to narrow search space\n4. **PCRE Overhead**: Regex is slower than literal content matching\n\n## Testing Rules\n\n1. **Syntax Check**:\n   ```bash\n   suricata -T -c /path/to/suricata.yaml -S /path/to/rules.rules\n   ```\n\n2. **Run with PCAP**:\n   ```bash\n   suricata -c /path/to/suricata.yaml -S /path/to/rules.rules -r traffic.pcap\n   ```\n\n3. **View Alerts**:\n   ```bash\n   cat /var/log/suricata/eve.json\n   tail -f /var/log/suricata/fast.log\n   ```\n\n## Common Patterns\n\n### Base64 Content Detection\nBase64 strings contain [A-Za-z0-9+/=] characters. Detection:\n```\npcre:\"/blob=([A-Za-z0-9+/]{80,})/\";\n```\n\n### Hex String Detection\nHex strings contain [0-9a-fA-F]:\n```\npcre:\"/sig=([0-9a-fA-F]{64})/\";\n```\n\n### Chaining Multiple Conditions\nUse multiple `content` keywords - all must match:\n```\nhttp.client_body; content:\"blob=\";\nhttp.client_body; content:\"sig=\"; distance:0;\n```\n\n## Useful Modifiers Summary\n\n| Modifier | Purpose |\n|----------|---------|\n| `nocase` | Case-insensitive |\n| `fast_pattern` | Primary pattern for performance |\n| `distance:N` | Within N bytes of previous match |\n| `offset:N` | Bytes from buffer start |\n| `within:N` | Must occur within N bytes |\n| `isdataat:N` | Assert data at offset |\n| `relative` | Relative to last match |\n| `bsize:N` | Buffer size is exactly N |\n\n## Next Steps\n- Review HTTP-specific detection patterns\n- Learn PCRE regex for complex patterns\n- Understand flow control for performance\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":5,"cache_creation_input_tokens":351,"cache_read_input_tokens":18785,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":351,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":3},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"cc03259f-759e-4298-9379-eb0c1e6395ed","uuid":"95394fb5-0da8-4ee9-a371-ea0e53dfaebe"}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001","id":"msg_vrtx_0163DTt4M5JhGdPdhQ3rXZiK","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01BNAvGipJ2XGtoZwEDDtugE","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/environment/skills/suricata-http-inspection.md","content":"# Suricata HTTP Content Inspection & Multi-Condition Matching\n\n## HTTP Request Structure Review\n\nA typical HTTP POST request:\n```\nPOST /telemetry/v2/report HTTP/1.1\nHost: example.com\nX-TLM-Mode: exfil\nContent-Type: application/x-www-form-urlencoded\nContent-Length: 250\n\nblob=SGVsbG8gV29ybGQgSXMgQSBMb25nIEJhc2U2NCBTdHJpbmc=&sig=abcd1234...\n```\n\n## HTTP Keyword Reference\n\n### Flow State\n```\nflow: established, to_server;\n```\n- `established` - TCP handshake completed\n- `to_server` - Inspection only on client->server direction\n- Best practice: Always use for POST detection to avoid false matches\n\n### Method Matching\n```\nhttp.method; content:\"POST\";\n```\n- Matches the HTTP method (GET, POST, PUT, DELETE, etc.)\n- Always include in POST-based rules\n\n### URI/Path Matching\n\n**Exact Path Match:**\n```\nhttp.uri; content:\"/telemetry/v2/report\"; exact;\n```\n- `exact` modifier ensures entire URI matches exactly\n- Without `exact`, `/telemetry/v2/report?foo=bar` would also match\n\n**Pattern Matching in Path:**\n```\nhttp.uri; content:\"/api/\"; startswith;\n```\n- `startswith` - Path starts with string\n- Can combine with regex for complex patterns\n\n### Header Matching\n\n**Basic Header Detection:**\n```\nhttp.header; content:\"X-TLM-Mode|3a| exfil\";\n```\n\n**Hex Encoding for Special Characters:**\n- Space: `|20|`\n- Colon `:`: `|3a|`\n- Semicolon `;`: `|3b|`\n- Equals `=`: `|3d|`\n- Newline `\\n`: `|0a|`\n- Carriage return `\\r`: `|0d|`\n\n**Header Value Extraction with PCRE:**\n```\nhttp.header; pcre:\"/X-TLM-Mode:\\s*([^;\\r\\n]+)/i\";\n```\n\n**Case-Insensitive Header (Recommended):**\n```\nhttp.header; content:\"x-tlm-mode|3a| exfil\"; nocase;\n```\n- HTTP headers are case-insensitive\n- `nocase` avoids missing matches like `X-TLM-MODE` or `x-tlm-mode`\n\n### Request Body Matching\n\n**Simple Content in Body:**\n```\nhttp.client_body; content:\"blob=\";\n```\n\n**Body with Specific Content Pattern:**\n```\nhttp.client_body; content:\"blob=\"; http.client_body; content:\"sig=\";\n```\n\n**Body Size Constraints:**\n```\nhttp.client_body; isdataat:200;\n```\n- Asserts body contains at least 200 bytes of data\n\n## Multi-Condition Matching Strategies\n\n### Sequential Content Matching (AND Logic)\n\nAll conditions must match (implicit AND):\n```\nalert http $HOME_NET any -> $EXTERNAL_NET any (\n    msg:\"POST with specific header and body\";\n    flow:established,to_server;\n    http.method; content:\"POST\";\n    http.uri; content:\"/api/endpoint\"; exact;\n    http.header; content:\"Authorization|3a|\";\n    http.client_body; content:\"action=\";\n    sid:1000001;\n)\n```\n\n### Content Ordering for Performance\n\nPut most specific patterns first:\n```\nalert http any any -> any any (\n    msg:\"Example\";\n    flow:established,to_server;\n    http.uri; content:\"/specific/path\"; exact;           # Very specific\n    http.header; content:\"X-Custom-Header|3a|\";          # Somewhat specific\n    http.client_body; content:\"key=value\";               # Less specific\n    http.method; content:\"POST\";                         # Generic\n    sid:1000001;\n)\n```\n\n### Using Distance for Ordered Patterns\n\nMatch one pattern, then another nearby:\n```\nhttp.client_body; content:\"blob=\"; fast_pattern;\nhttp.client_body; content:\"sig=\"; distance:0; within:200;\n```\n- `distance:0` - `sig=` appears after `blob=`\n- `within:200` - `sig=` within 200 bytes of `blob=`\n- `fast_pattern` - Use `blob=` for initial fast matching\n\n### Using Regex for Complex Patterns\n\n**Base64-like Content (≥80 chars):**\n```\nhttp.client_body; pcre:\"/blob=([A-Za-z0-9+/]{80,})/\";\n```\n- Matches `blob=` followed by 80+ Base64 characters\n- Base64 alphabet: A-Z, a-z, 0-9, +, /\n- Padding with `=` also allowed: `[A-Za-z0-9+/=]{80,}`\n\n**Exactly 64 Hex Digits:**\n```\nhttp.client_body; pcre:\"/sig=([0-9a-fA-F]{64})/\";\n```\n- Matches `sig=` followed by exactly 64 hex characters (256-bit signature)\n- `[0-9a-fA-F]` matches valid hex digits\n- `{64}` ensures exactly 64 characters\n\n**Combined Base64 and Hex:**\n```\nhttp.client_body; pcre:\"/blob=([A-Za-z0-9+/=]{80,}).*sig=([0-9a-fA-F]{64})/s\";\n```\n- `/s` flag makes `.` match newlines\n- Ensure both patterns exist in body\n\n## Avoiding False Positives\n\n### 1. Use Exact Matching\n```\nhttp.uri; content:\"/exact/path\"; exact;\n```\n- Prevents matching `/exact/path/subdir`\n- Without `exact`, substring matches trigger alerts\n\n### 2. Specify Direction\n```\nflow: established, to_server;\n```\n- Only inspect client->server traffic\n- Prevents matching server responses\n\n### 3. Use Case-Insensitive Only When Needed\n```\nhttp.header; content:\"x-custom|3a|\"; nocase;\n```\n- HTTP headers are case-insensitive\n- Use `nocase` for headers, not for body content unless needed\n\n### 4. Validate Content Patterns\n```\nhttp.client_body; pcre:\"/blob=([A-Za-z0-9+/=]{80,})/\";\n```\n- Use specific character classes\n- Avoid overly broad patterns like `blob=.{80,}` which matches any 80 chars\n\n### 5. Combine Multiple Conditions\n```\nhttp.method; content:\"POST\";\nhttp.uri; content:\"/telemetry/v2/report\"; exact;\nhttp.header; content:\"X-TLM-Mode|3a| exfil\";\n```\n- Each condition narrows search space\n- Reduces false positives through specificity\n\n### 6. Test Against Normal Traffic\n- Run rule against clean PCAP files\n- Verify no alerts on legitimate requests\n- Check edge cases (different header casing, URL parameters)\n\n## Performance Tips\n\n1. **Fast Pattern**: Always mark most specific pattern\n   ```\n   http.uri; content:\"/exact/path\"; exact; fast_pattern;\n   ```\n\n2. **Order by Specificity**:\n   - Method (generic) → URI (specific) → Headers (specific) → Body (less specific)\n\n3. **Avoid Complex Regex**: Use literal `content` when possible\n   ```\n   # Fast\n   http.client_body; content:\"blob=\";\n\n   # Slower\n   http.client_body; pcre:\"/blob=/\";\n   ```\n\n4. **Pre-filter with Flow**:\n   ```\n   flow: established, to_server;\n   ```\n   - Eliminates non-POST requests early\n\n## Example: Complete HTTP Inspection Rule\n\n```\nalert http $HOME_NET any -> $EXTERNAL_NET any (\n    msg:\"POST data exfiltration via telemetry\";\n    flow: established, to_server;\n    http.method; content:\"POST\";\n    http.uri; content:\"/telemetry/v2/report\"; exact;\n    http.header; content:\"x-tlm-mode|3a|\"; nocase;\n    http.header; content:\"exfil\"; nocase; distance:0;\n    http.client_body; content:\"blob=\"; fast_pattern;\n    http.client_body; pcre:\"/blob=([A-Za-z0-9+/=]{80,})/\";\n    http.client_body; pcre:\"/sig=([0-9a-fA-F]{64})/\";\n    sid:1000001;\n    rev:1;\n)\n```\n\n## Debugging Tips\n\n### Check Rule Syntax\n```bash\nsuricata -T -c suricata.yaml -S local.rules\n```\n\n### Test Rule with PCAP\n```bash\nsuricata -c suricata.yaml -S local.rules -r traffic.pcap -l output/\n```\n\n### View Alerts\n```bash\ncat output/eve.json | jq . | grep -A5 \"alert\"\ntail -f output/fast.log\n```\n\n### Enable Debug for HTTP\nAdd to suricata.yaml:\n```yaml\nlogging:\n  outputs:\n  - eve-log:\n      enabled: yes\n      types:\n        - http\n```\n\n## Common Mistakes\n\n1. **Missing `exact` on URI**: Matches `/path` and `/path123`\n2. **Not using `nocase` for headers**: Headers are case-insensitive\n3. **Wrong hex encoding**: Remember `|3a|` = `:`, not `;`\n4. **Forgetting `to_server`**: Can match bidirectional traffic\n5. **Overly broad regex**: `.*` instead of specific character classes\n6. **Not validating base64**: `[A-Za-z0-9+/=]` vs generic `.*`\n\n## Next Steps\n- Learn packet analysis tools for traffic investigation\n- Understand Suricata configuration for proper logging\n- Practice with real-world PCAP files\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":5,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1683,"cache_read_input_tokens":19136,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1683,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":6},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"cc03259f-759e-4298-9379-eb0c1e6395ed","uuid":"bc5fae6b-2bce-46f3-908a-c27412317242"}
{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_vrtx_01BNAvGipJ2XGtoZwEDDtugE","type":"tool_result","content":"File created successfully at: /root/environment/skills/suricata-http-inspection.md"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"cc03259f-759e-4298-9379-eb0c1e6395ed","uuid":"a59c6114-c2c7-4177-bc46-c58160500f9f","tool_use_result":{"type":"create","filePath":"/root/environment/skills/suricata-http-inspection.md","content":"# Suricata HTTP Content Inspection & Multi-Condition Matching\n\n## HTTP Request Structure Review\n\nA typical HTTP POST request:\n```\nPOST /telemetry/v2/report HTTP/1.1\nHost: example.com\nX-TLM-Mode: exfil\nContent-Type: application/x-www-form-urlencoded\nContent-Length: 250\n\nblob=SGVsbG8gV29ybGQgSXMgQSBMb25nIEJhc2U2NCBTdHJpbmc=&sig=abcd1234...\n```\n\n## HTTP Keyword Reference\n\n### Flow State\n```\nflow: established, to_server;\n```\n- `established` - TCP handshake completed\n- `to_server` - Inspection only on client->server direction\n- Best practice: Always use for POST detection to avoid false matches\n\n### Method Matching\n```\nhttp.method; content:\"POST\";\n```\n- Matches the HTTP method (GET, POST, PUT, DELETE, etc.)\n- Always include in POST-based rules\n\n### URI/Path Matching\n\n**Exact Path Match:**\n```\nhttp.uri; content:\"/telemetry/v2/report\"; exact;\n```\n- `exact` modifier ensures entire URI matches exactly\n- Without `exact`, `/telemetry/v2/report?foo=bar` would also match\n\n**Pattern Matching in Path:**\n```\nhttp.uri; content:\"/api/\"; startswith;\n```\n- `startswith` - Path starts with string\n- Can combine with regex for complex patterns\n\n### Header Matching\n\n**Basic Header Detection:**\n```\nhttp.header; content:\"X-TLM-Mode|3a| exfil\";\n```\n\n**Hex Encoding for Special Characters:**\n- Space: `|20|`\n- Colon `:`: `|3a|`\n- Semicolon `;`: `|3b|`\n- Equals `=`: `|3d|`\n- Newline `\\n`: `|0a|`\n- Carriage return `\\r`: `|0d|`\n\n**Header Value Extraction with PCRE:**\n```\nhttp.header; pcre:\"/X-TLM-Mode:\\s*([^;\\r\\n]+)/i\";\n```\n\n**Case-Insensitive Header (Recommended):**\n```\nhttp.header; content:\"x-tlm-mode|3a| exfil\"; nocase;\n```\n- HTTP headers are case-insensitive\n- `nocase` avoids missing matches like `X-TLM-MODE` or `x-tlm-mode`\n\n### Request Body Matching\n\n**Simple Content in Body:**\n```\nhttp.client_body; content:\"blob=\";\n```\n\n**Body with Specific Content Pattern:**\n```\nhttp.client_body; content:\"blob=\"; http.client_body; content:\"sig=\";\n```\n\n**Body Size Constraints:**\n```\nhttp.client_body; isdataat:200;\n```\n- Asserts body contains at least 200 bytes of data\n\n## Multi-Condition Matching Strategies\n\n### Sequential Content Matching (AND Logic)\n\nAll conditions must match (implicit AND):\n```\nalert http $HOME_NET any -> $EXTERNAL_NET any (\n    msg:\"POST with specific header and body\";\n    flow:established,to_server;\n    http.method; content:\"POST\";\n    http.uri; content:\"/api/endpoint\"; exact;\n    http.header; content:\"Authorization|3a|\";\n    http.client_body; content:\"action=\";\n    sid:1000001;\n)\n```\n\n### Content Ordering for Performance\n\nPut most specific patterns first:\n```\nalert http any any -> any any (\n    msg:\"Example\";\n    flow:established,to_server;\n    http.uri; content:\"/specific/path\"; exact;           # Very specific\n    http.header; content:\"X-Custom-Header|3a|\";          # Somewhat specific\n    http.client_body; content:\"key=value\";               # Less specific\n    http.method; content:\"POST\";                         # Generic\n    sid:1000001;\n)\n```\n\n### Using Distance for Ordered Patterns\n\nMatch one pattern, then another nearby:\n```\nhttp.client_body; content:\"blob=\"; fast_pattern;\nhttp.client_body; content:\"sig=\"; distance:0; within:200;\n```\n- `distance:0` - `sig=` appears after `blob=`\n- `within:200` - `sig=` within 200 bytes of `blob=`\n- `fast_pattern` - Use `blob=` for initial fast matching\n\n### Using Regex for Complex Patterns\n\n**Base64-like Content (≥80 chars):**\n```\nhttp.client_body; pcre:\"/blob=([A-Za-z0-9+/]{80,})/\";\n```\n- Matches `blob=` followed by 80+ Base64 characters\n- Base64 alphabet: A-Z, a-z, 0-9, +, /\n- Padding with `=` also allowed: `[A-Za-z0-9+/=]{80,}`\n\n**Exactly 64 Hex Digits:**\n```\nhttp.client_body; pcre:\"/sig=([0-9a-fA-F]{64})/\";\n```\n- Matches `sig=` followed by exactly 64 hex characters (256-bit signature)\n- `[0-9a-fA-F]` matches valid hex digits\n- `{64}` ensures exactly 64 characters\n\n**Combined Base64 and Hex:**\n```\nhttp.client_body; pcre:\"/blob=([A-Za-z0-9+/=]{80,}).*sig=([0-9a-fA-F]{64})/s\";\n```\n- `/s` flag makes `.` match newlines\n- Ensure both patterns exist in body\n\n## Avoiding False Positives\n\n### 1. Use Exact Matching\n```\nhttp.uri; content:\"/exact/path\"; exact;\n```\n- Prevents matching `/exact/path/subdir`\n- Without `exact`, substring matches trigger alerts\n\n### 2. Specify Direction\n```\nflow: established, to_server;\n```\n- Only inspect client->server traffic\n- Prevents matching server responses\n\n### 3. Use Case-Insensitive Only When Needed\n```\nhttp.header; content:\"x-custom|3a|\"; nocase;\n```\n- HTTP headers are case-insensitive\n- Use `nocase` for headers, not for body content unless needed\n\n### 4. Validate Content Patterns\n```\nhttp.client_body; pcre:\"/blob=([A-Za-z0-9+/=]{80,})/\";\n```\n- Use specific character classes\n- Avoid overly broad patterns like `blob=.{80,}` which matches any 80 chars\n\n### 5. Combine Multiple Conditions\n```\nhttp.method; content:\"POST\";\nhttp.uri; content:\"/telemetry/v2/report\"; exact;\nhttp.header; content:\"X-TLM-Mode|3a| exfil\";\n```\n- Each condition narrows search space\n- Reduces false positives through specificity\n\n### 6. Test Against Normal Traffic\n- Run rule against clean PCAP files\n- Verify no alerts on legitimate requests\n- Check edge cases (different header casing, URL parameters)\n\n## Performance Tips\n\n1. **Fast Pattern**: Always mark most specific pattern\n   ```\n   http.uri; content:\"/exact/path\"; exact; fast_pattern;\n   ```\n\n2. **Order by Specificity**:\n   - Method (generic) → URI (specific) → Headers (specific) → Body (less specific)\n\n3. **Avoid Complex Regex**: Use literal `content` when possible\n   ```\n   # Fast\n   http.client_body; content:\"blob=\";\n\n   # Slower\n   http.client_body; pcre:\"/blob=/\";\n   ```\n\n4. **Pre-filter with Flow**:\n   ```\n   flow: established, to_server;\n   ```\n   - Eliminates non-POST requests early\n\n## Example: Complete HTTP Inspection Rule\n\n```\nalert http $HOME_NET any -> $EXTERNAL_NET any (\n    msg:\"POST data exfiltration via telemetry\";\n    flow: established, to_server;\n    http.method; content:\"POST\";\n    http.uri; content:\"/telemetry/v2/report\"; exact;\n    http.header; content:\"x-tlm-mode|3a|\"; nocase;\n    http.header; content:\"exfil\"; nocase; distance:0;\n    http.client_body; content:\"blob=\"; fast_pattern;\n    http.client_body; pcre:\"/blob=([A-Za-z0-9+/=]{80,})/\";\n    http.client_body; pcre:\"/sig=([0-9a-fA-F]{64})/\";\n    sid:1000001;\n    rev:1;\n)\n```\n\n## Debugging Tips\n\n### Check Rule Syntax\n```bash\nsuricata -T -c suricata.yaml -S local.rules\n```\n\n### Test Rule with PCAP\n```bash\nsuricata -c suricata.yaml -S local.rules -r traffic.pcap -l output/\n```\n\n### View Alerts\n```bash\ncat output/eve.json | jq . | grep -A5 \"alert\"\ntail -f output/fast.log\n```\n\n### Enable Debug for HTTP\nAdd to suricata.yaml:\n```yaml\nlogging:\n  outputs:\n  - eve-log:\n      enabled: yes\n      types:\n        - http\n```\n\n## Common Mistakes\n\n1. **Missing `exact` on URI**: Matches `/path` and `/path123`\n2. **Not using `nocase` for headers**: Headers are case-insensitive\n3. **Wrong hex encoding**: Remember `|3a|` = `:`, not `;`\n4. **Forgetting `to_server`**: Can match bidirectional traffic\n5. **Overly broad regex**: `.*` instead of specific character classes\n6. **Not validating base64**: `[A-Za-z0-9+/=]` vs generic `.*`\n\n## Next Steps\n- Learn packet analysis tools for traffic investigation\n- Understand Suricata configuration for proper logging\n- Practice with real-world PCAP files\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

Syntax Validation\n\nBefore testing against network traffic, validate rule syntax:\n\n```bash\nsuricata -T -c /path/to/suricata.yaml -S /path/to/rules.rules\n```\n\nOutput should show:\n```\n[PASS] Syntax check passed\n```\n\nIf there are errors:\n```\n[ERROR] Parsing error in rule file at line N\n```\n\n### 2. Test with PCAP Files\n\nRun Suricata against captured traffic:\n\n```bash\nsuricata -c /path/to/suricata.yaml -S /path/to/rules.rules -r input.pcap -l output_dir/\n```\n\nParameters:\n- `-c` - Configuration file\n- `-S` - Rules file\n- `-r` - Input PCAP file\n- `-l` - Output directory for logs\n\n### 3. View Alert Output\n\n#### EVE JSON Format (Structured)\n```bash\ncat output_dir/eve.json | jq '.[] | select(.event_type==\"alert\")'\n```\n\nExample output:\n```json\n{\n  \"timestamp\": \"2024-01-15T10:23:45.123456+0000\",\n  \"flow_id\": 1234567890,\n  \"event_type\": \"alert\",\n  \"alert\": {\n    \"action\": \"alert\",\n    \"gid\": 1,\n    \"signature_id\": 1000001,\n    \"rev\": 1,\n    \"signature\": \"POST data exfiltration\",\n    \"category\": \"Attempted Information Leak\",\n    \"severity\": 3\n  },\n  \"http\": {\n    \"hostname\": \"example.com\",\n    \"url\": \"/telemetry/v2/report\",\n    \"http_method\": \"POST\",\n    \"protocol\": \"HTTP/1.1\",\n    \"status\": 200,\n    \"length\": 250\n  }\n}\n```\n\n#### Fast Log Format (Plain Text)\n```bash\ncat output_dir/fast.log\n```\n\nExample:\n```\n01/15/2024-10:23:45.123456 [**] [1:1000001:1] POST data exfiltration [**] [Classification: Attempted Information Leak] [Priority: 3] {TCP} 192.168.1.100:52341 -> 10.0.0.50:80\n```\n\n### 4. Analyze Specific Alerts\n\nFilter alerts by signature ID:\n```bash\ncat output_dir/eve.json | jq '.[] | select(.alert.signature_id==1000001)'\n```\n\nGet counts of alerts:\n```bash\ncat output_dir/eve.json | jq -r '.alert.signature_id' | sort | uniq -c\n```\n\n## False Positive Testing\n\n### What to Test Against\n\n1. **Normal traffic** - Legitimate POST requests to different endpoints\n2. **Similar endpoints** - POST to `/telemetry/v1/report`, `/telemetry/v3/report`\n3. **Different headers** - Valid X-TLM-Mode values (not \"exfil\")\n4. **Edge cases** - Base64 < 80 chars, hex signatures not exactly 64 chars\n\n### Creating Test PCAP\n\nUse Python with `scapy`:\n```python\nfrom scapy.all import IP, TCP, Raw\nfrom scapy.layers.inet import IP, TCP\nfrom scapy.layers.l2 import Ether\n\n# Create packet\npkt = Ether()/IP(dst=\"10.0.0.50\")/TCP(dport=80)/Raw(load=b\"GET / HTTP/1.1\\r\\n...\")\npkt.show()\n```\n\nOr use command-line tools:\n```bash\n# With tcpdump (passive capture)\ntcpdump -i eth0 -w output.pcap\n\n# Replay existing PCAP\ntcpreplay -i eth0 input.pcap\n```\n\n### Example False Positive Tests\n\n**Test 1: Legitimate telemetry (should NOT alert)**\n```\nPOST /telemetry/v2/report HTTP/1.1\nX-TLM-Mode: normal\nContent-Type: application/x-www-form-urlencoded\n\nblob=short&sig=notahexstring\n```\nExpected: No alert (blob < 80 chars, sig not 64 hex digits)\n\n**Test 2: Different endpoint (should NOT alert)**\n```\nPOST /telemetry/v1/report HTTP/1.1\nX-TLM-Mode: exfil\nContent-Type: application/x-www-form-urlencoded\n\nblob=SGVsbG8gV29ybGQgSXMgQSBMb25nIEJhc2U2NCBTdHJpbmc...&sig=abcd1234...\n```\nExpected: No alert (wrong path)\n\n**Test 3: Different header value (should NOT alert)**\n```\nPOST /telemetry/v2/report HTTP/1.1\nX-TLM-Mode: normal\nContent-Type: application/x-www-form-urlencoded\n\nblob=SGVsbG8gV29ybGQgSXMgQSBMb25nIEJhc2U2NCBTdHJpbmc...&sig=abcd1234...\n```\nExpected: No alert (header value not \"exfil\")\n\n## Debugging Failed Rules\n\n### Enable Verbose Logging\n\nCreate a custom suricata.yaml for testing:\n```yaml\nlogging:\n  default-log-level: info\n  default-output-format: \"[%i] %d -- [%n] [%s]\"\n\n  outputs:\n  - fast:\n      enabled: yes\n      filename: fast.log\n  - eve-log:\n      enabled: yes\n      filename: eve.json\n      types:\n        - alert\n        - http\n        - flow\n\napp-layer:\n  protocols:\n    http:\n      enabled: yes\n```\n\n### Common Issues & Solutions\n\n**Issue: Rule not triggering on expected traffic**\n\n1. Check flow direction:\n   ```\n   # Wrong - may not match all scenarios\n   flow: to_server;\n\n   # Better - established connection\n   flow: established, to_server;\n   ```\n\n2. Verify header matching:\n   ```bash\n   # Debug: Check if header is present in traffic\n   tcpdump -i eth0 -A 'tcp port 80' | grep -i \"X-TLM-Mode\"\n   ```\n\n3. Check for encoding issues:\n   ```\n   # Header value might have spaces or special chars\n   http.header; content:\"x-tlm-mode|3a|\"; nocase;\n   http.header; content:\"exfil\"; nocase; distance:0;\n   ```\n\n4. Verify body patterns:\n   ```bash\n   # Analyze actual body content\n   tcpdump -i eth0 -A 'tcp port 80' | grep -A5 \"POST\"\n   ```\n\n**Issue: Too many false positives**\n\n1. Add more specific conditions:\n   ```\n   # Too broad\n   http.client_body; content:\"blob=\";\n\n   # Better - requires base64 pattern\n   http.client_body; pcre:\"/blob=([A-Za-z0-9+/=]{80,})/\";\n   ```\n\n2. Use exact path matching:\n   ```\n   # Wrong - matches /path and /path123\n   http.uri; content:\"/telemetry/v2/report\";\n\n   # Right\n   http.uri; content:\"/telemetry/v2/report\"; exact;\n   ```\n\n### Packet Analysis for Verification\n\nUse tcpdump to capture and inspect traffic:\n\n```bash\n# Capture HTTP traffic\ntcpdump -i eth0 -A 'tcp port 80' -w http_traffic.pcap\n\n# View captured packets\ntcpdump -r http_traffic.pcap -A\n\n# Filter for specific pattern\ntcpdump -r traffic.pcap -A 'dst 10.0.0.50' | grep -i \"telemetry\"\n```\n\n### Regex Testing\n\nTest PCRE patterns independently:\n```bash\n# Using grep\necho \"blob=SGVsbG8gV29ybGQgSXMgQSBMb25nIEJhc2U2NCBTdHJpbmc=\" | grep -E \"blob=([A-Za-z0-9+/=]{80,})\"\n\n# Using Python\nimport re\npattern = r\"blob=([A-Za-z0-9+/=]{80,})\"\ntext = \"blob=SGVsbG8gV29ybGQgSXMgQSBMb25nIEJhc2U2NCBTdHJpbmc=\"\nmatch = re.search(pattern, text)\nprint(match.group(0) if match else \"No match\")\n```\n\n## Performance Validation\n\n### Check Rule Efficiency\n\nReview warning messages:\n```bash\nsuricata -T -c suricata.yaml -S local.rules 2>&1 | grep -i warning\n```\n\nLook for:\n- Missing `fast_pattern` warnings\n- Complex regex that should be literal content\n- Inefficient pattern ordering\n\n### Measure Performance\n\nRun with timing:\n```bash\ntime suricata -c suricata.yaml -S local.rules -r large_traffic.pcap\n```\n\nExpected output shows:\n- Processing time\n- Packets processed per second\n- Any performance warnings\n\n## Validation Checklist\n\nBefore deployment, verify:\n\n- [ ] Syntax validation passes (`suricata -T`)\n- [ ] Rule triggers on intended exfil traffic (true positives)\n- [ ] Rule does NOT trigger on legitimate traffic (false positive testing)\n- [ ] Path matching uses `exact` keyword\n- [ ] Headers use `nocase` for case-insensitivity\n- [ ] Base64 pattern requires ≥80 characters\n- [ ] Hex pattern requires exactly 64 characters\n- [ ] `flow: established, to_server` is set\n- [ ] `http.method; Analyze Specific Alerts\n\nFilter alerts by signature ID:\n```bash\ncat output_dir/eve.json | jq '.[] | select(.alert.signature_id==1000001)'\n```\n\nGet counts of alerts:\n```bash\ncat output_dir/eve.json | jq -r '.alert.signature_id' | sort | uniq -c\n```\n\n## False Positive Testing\n\n### What to Test Against\n\n1. **Normal traffic** - Legitimate POST requests to different endpoints\n2. **Similar endpoints** - POST to `/telemetry/v1/report`, `/telemetry/v3/report`\n3. **Different headers** - Valid X-TLM-Mode values (not \"exfil\")\n4. **Edge cases** - Base64 < 80 chars, hex signatures not exactly 64 chars\n\n### Creating Test PCAP\n\nUse Python with `scapy`:\n```python\nfrom scapy.all import IP, TCP, Raw\nfrom scapy.layers.inet import IP, TCP\nfrom scapy.layers.l2 import Ether\n\n# Create packet\npkt = Ether()/IP(dst=\"10.0.0.50\")/TCP(dport=80)/Raw(load=b\"GET / HTTP/1.1\\r\\n...\")\npkt.show()\n```\n\nOr use command-line tools:\n```bash\n# With tcpdump (passive capture)\ntcpdump -i eth0 -w output.pcap\n\n# Replay existing PCAP\ntcpreplay -i eth0 input.pcap\n```\n\n### Example False Positive Tests\n\n**Test 1: Legitimate telemetry (should NOT alert)**\n```\nPOST /telemetry/v2/report HTTP/1.1\nX-TLM-Mode: normal\nContent-Type: application/x-www-form-urlencoded\n\nblob=short&sig=notahexstring\n```\nExpected: No alert (blob < 80 chars, sig not 64 hex digits)\n\n**Test 2: Different endpoint (should NOT alert)**\n```\nPOST /telemetry/v1/report HTTP/1.1\nX-TLM-Mode: exfil\nContent-Type: application/x-www-form-urlencoded\n\nblob=SGVsbG8gV29ybGQgSXMgQSBMb25nIEJhc2U2NCBTdHJpbmc...&sig=abcd1234...\n```\nExpected: No alert (wrong path)\n\n**Test 3: Different header value (should NOT alert)**\n```\nPOST /telemetry/v2/report HTTP/1.1\nX-TLM-Mode: normal\nContent-Type: application/x-www-form-urlencoded\n\nblob=SGVsbG8gV29ybGQgSXMgQSBMb25nIEJhc2U2NCBTdHJpbmc...&sig=abcd1234...\n```\nExpected: No alert (header value not \"exfil\")\n\n## Debugging Failed Rules\n\n### Enable Verbose Logging\n\nCreate a custom suricata.yaml for testing:\n```yaml\nlogging:\n  default-log-level: info\n  default-output-format: \"[%i] %d -- [%n] [%s]\"\n\n  outputs:\n  - fast:\n      enabled: yes\n      filename: fast.log\n  - eve-log:\n      enabled: yes\n      filename: eve.json\n      types:\n        - alert\n        - http\n        - flow\n\napp-layer:\n  protocols:\n    http:\n      enabled: yes\n```\n\n### Common Issues & Solutions\n\n**Issue: Rule not triggering on expected traffic**\n\n1. Check flow direction:\n   ```\n   # Wrong - may not match all scenarios\n   flow: to_server;\n\n   # Better - established connection\n   flow: established, to_server;\n   ```\n\n2. Verify header matching:\n   ```bash\n   # Debug: Check if header is present in traffic\n   tcpdump -i eth0 -A 'tcp port 80' | grep -i \"X-TLM-Mode\"\n   ```\n\n3. Check for encoding issues:\n   ```\n   # Header value might have spaces or special chars\n   http.header; content:\"x-tlm-mode|3a|\"; nocase;\n   http.header; content:\"exfil\"; nocase; distance:0;\n   ```\n\n4. Verify body patterns:\n   ```bash\n   # Analyze actual body content\n   tcpdump -i eth0 -A 'tcp port 80' | grep -A5 \"POST\"\n   ```\n\n**Issue: Too many false positives**\n\n1. Add more specific conditions:\n   ```\n   # Too broad\n   http.client_body; content:\"blob=\";\n\n   # Better - requires base64 pattern\n   http.client_body; pcre:\"/blob=([A-Za-z0-9+/=]{80,})/\";\n   ```\n\n2. Use exact path matching:\n   ```\n   # Wrong - matches /path and /path123\n   http.uri; content:\"/telemetry/v2/report\";\n\n   # Right\n   http.uri; content:\"/telemetry/v2/report\"; exact;\n   ```\n\n### Packet Analysis for Verification\n\nUse tcpdump to capture and inspect traffic:\n\n```bash\n# Capture HTTP traffic\ntcpdump -i eth0 -A 'tcp port 80' -w http_traffic.pcap\n\n# View captured packets\ntcpdump -r http_traffic.pcap -A\n\n# Filter for specific pattern\ntcpdump -r traffic.pcap -A 'dst 10.0.0.50' | grep -i \"telemetry\"\n```\n\n### Regex Testing\n\nTest PCRE patterns independently:\n```bash\n# Using grep\necho \"blob=SGVsbG8gV29ybGQgSXMgQSBMb25nIEJhc2U2NCBTdHJpbmc=\" | grep -E \"blob=([A-Za-z0-9+/=]{80,})\"\n\n# Using Python\nimport re\npattern = r\"blob=([A-Za-z0-9+/=]{80,})\"\ntext = \"blob=SGVsbG8gV29ybGQgSXMgQSBMb25nIEJhc2U2NCBTdHJpbmc=\"\nmatch = re.search(pattern, text)\nprint(match.group(0) if match else \"No match\")\n```\n\n## Performance Validation\n\n### Check Rule Efficiency\n\nReview warning messages:\n```bash\nsuricata -T -c suricata.yaml -S local.rules 2>&1 | grep -i warning\n```\n\nLook for:\n- Missing `fast_pattern` warnings\n- Complex regex that should be literal content\n- Inefficient pattern ordering\n\n### Measure Performance\n\nRun with timing:\n```bash\ntime suricata -c suricata.yaml -S local.rules -r large_traffic.pcap\n```\n\nExpected output shows:\n- Processing time\n- Packets processed per second\n- Any performance warnings\n\n## Validation Checklist\n\nBefore deployment, verify:\n\n- [ ] Syntax validation passes (`suricata -T`)\n- [ ] Rule triggers on intended exfil traffic (true positives)\n- [ ] Rule does NOT trigger on legitimate traffic (false positive testing)\n- [ ] Path matching uses `exact` keyword\n- [ ] Headers use `nocase` for case-insensitivity\n- [ ] Base64 pattern requires ≥80 characters\n- [ ] Hex pattern requires exactly 64 characters\n- [ ] `flow: established, to_server` is set\n- [ ] `http.method; content:\"POST\"` is present\n- [ ] Performance is acceptable (<1% CPU overhead)\n- [ ] Alert output format is correct (EVE JSON or fast.log)\n\n## Integration Testing\n\nOnce rule is validated, integrate into production:\n\n1. Here you can specify the timeouts that the\n# active flows will wait to transit from the current state to another, on each\n# protocol. The value of \"new\" determines the seconds to wait after a handshake or\n# stream startup before the engine frees the data of that flow it doesn't\n# change the state to established (usually if we don't receive more packets\n# of that flow). The value of \"established\" is the amount of\n# seconds that the engine will wait to free the flow if that time elapses\n# without receiving new packets or closing the connection. \"closed\" is the\n# amount of time to wait after a flow is closed (usually zero). \"bypassed\"\n# timeout controls locally bypassed flows. For these flows we don't do any other\n# tracking. If no packets have been seen after this timeout, the flow is discarded.\n#\n# There's an emergency mode that will become active under attack circumstances,\n# making the engine to check flow status faster. This configuration variables\n# use the prefix \"emergency-\" and work similar as the normal ones.\n# Some timeouts doesn't apply to all the protocols, like \"closed\", for udp and\n# icmp.\n\nflow-timeouts:\n\n  default:\n    new: 30\n    established: 300\n    closed: 0\n    bypassed: 100\n    emergency-new: 10\n    emergency-established: 100\n    emergency-closed: 0\n    emergency-bypassed: 50\n  tcp:\n    new: 60\n    established: 600\n    closed: 60\n    bypassed: 100\n    emergency-new: 5\n    emergency-established: 100\n    emergency-closed: 10\n    emergency-bypassed: 50\n  udp:\n    new: 30\n    established: 300\n    bypassed: 100\n    emergency-new: 10\n    emergency-established: 100\n    emergency-bypassed: 50\n  icmp:\n    new: 30\n    established: 300\n    bypassed: 100\n    emergency-new: 10\n    emergency-established: 100\n    emergency-bypassed: 50\n\n# Stream engine settings. Here the TCP stream tracking and reassembly\n# engine is configured.\n#\n# stream:\n#   memcap: 64mb                # Can be specified in kb, mb, gb.  Just a\n#                               # number indicates it's in bytes.\n#   memcap-policy: ignore       # Can be \"drop-flow\", \"pass-flow\", \"bypass\",\n#                               # \"drop-packet\", \"pass-packet\", \"reject\" or\n#                               # \"ignore\" default is \"ignore\"\n#   checksum-validation: yes    # To validate the checksum of received\n#                               # packet. If csum validation is specified as\n#                               # \"yes\", then packets with invalid csum values will not\n#                               # be processed by the engine stream/app layer.\n#                               # Warning: locally generated traffic can be\n#                               # generated without checksum due to hardware offload\n#                               # of checksum. You can control the handling of checksum\n#                               # on a per-interface basis via the 'checksum-checks'\n#                               # option\n#   prealloc-sessions: 2048     # 2k sessions prealloc'd per stream thread\n#   midstream: false            # don't allow midstream session pickups\n#   midstream-policy: ignore    # Can be \"drop-flow\", \"pass-flow\", \"bypass\",\n#                               # \"drop-packet\", \"pass-packet\", \"reject\" or\n#                               # \"ignore\" default is \"ignore\"\n#   async-oneside: false        # don't enable async stream handling\n#   inline: no                  # stream inline mode\n#   drop-invalid: yes           # in inline mode, drop packets that are invalid with regards to streaming engine\n#   max-syn-queued: 10          # Max different SYNs to queue\n#   max-synack-queued: 5        # Max different SYN/ACKs to queue\n#   bypass: no                  # Bypass packets when stream.reassembly.depth is reached.\n#                               # Warning: first side to reach this triggers\n#                               # the bypass.\n#   liberal-timestamps: false   # Treat all timestamps as if the Linux policy applies. This\n#                               # means it's slightly more permissive. Enabled by default.\n#\n#   reassembly:\n#     memcap: 256mb             # Can be specified in kb, mb, gb.  Just a number\n#                               # indicates it's in bytes.\n#     memcap-policy: ignore     # Can be \"drop-flow\", \"pass-flow\", \"bypass\",\n#                               # \"drop-packet\", \"pass-packet\", \"reject\" or\n#                               # \"ignore\" default is \"ignore\"\n#     depth: 1mb                # Can be specified in kb, mb, gb.  Just a number\n#                               # indicates it's in bytes.\n#     toserver-chunk-size: 2560 # inspect raw stream in chunks of at least\n#                               # this size.  Can be specified in kb, mb,\n#                               # gb.  Just a number indicates it's in bytes.\n#     toclient-chunk-size: 2560 # inspect raw stream in chunks of at least\n#                               # this size.  Can be specified in kb, mb,\n#                               # gb.  Just a number indicates it's in bytes.\n#     randomize-chunk-size: yes # Take a random value for chunk size around the specified value.\n#                               # This lowers the risk of some evasion techniques but could lead\n#                               # to detection change between runs. It is set to 'yes' by default.\n#     randomize-chunk-range: 10 # If randomize-chunk-size is active, the value of chunk-size is\n#                               # a random value between (1 - randomize-chunk-range/100)*toserver-chunk-size\n#                               # and (1 + randomize-chunk-range/100)*toserver-chunk-size and the same\n#                               # calculation for toclient-chunk-size.\n#                               # Default value of randomize-chunk-range is 10.\n#\n#     raw: yes                  # 'Raw' reassembly enabled or disabled.\n#                               # raw is for content inspection by detection\n#                               # engine.\n#\n#     segment-prealloc: 2048    # number of segments preallocated per thread\n#\n#     check-overlap-different-data: true|false\n#                               # check if a segment contains different data\n#                               # than what we've already seen for that\n#                               # position in the stream.\n#                               # This is enabled automatically if inline mode\n#                               # is used or when stream-event:reassembly_overlap_different_data;\n#                               # is used in a rule.\n#\nstream:\n  memcap: 64mb\n  #memcap-policy: ignore\n  checksum-validation: yes      # reject incorrect csums\n  #midstream: false\n  #midstream-policy: ignore\n  inline: auto                  # auto will use inline mode in IPS mode, yes or no set it statically\n  reassembly:\n    # experimental TCP urgent handling logic\n    #urgent:\n    #  policy: inline           # drop, inline, oob (1 byte, see RFC 6093, 3.1), gap\n    #  oob-limit-policy: drop\n    memcap: 256mb\n    #memcap-policy: ignore\n    depth: 1mb                  # reassemble 1mb into a stream\n    toserver-chunk-size: 2560\n    toclient-chunk-size: 2560\n    randomize-chunk-size: yes\n    #randomize-chunk-range: 10\n    #raw: yes\n    #segment-prealloc: 2048\n    #check-overlap-different-data: true\n\n# Host table:\n#\n# Host table is used by the tagging and per host thresholding subsystems.\n#\nhost:\n  hash-size: 4096\n  prealloc: 1000\n  memcap: 32mb\n\n# IP Pair table:\n#\n# Used by xbits 'ippair' tracking.\n#\n#ippair:\n#  hash-size: 4096\n#  prealloc: 1000\n#  memcap: 32mb\n\n# Decoder settings\n\ndecoder:\n  # Teredo decoder is known to not be completely accurate\n  # as it will sometimes detect non-teredo as teredo.\n  teredo:\n    enabled: true\n    # ports to look for Teredo. Max 4 ports. If no ports are given, or\n    # the value is set to 'any', Teredo detection runs on _all_ UDP packets.\n    ports: $TEREDO_PORTS # syntax: '[3544, 1234]' or '3533' or 'any'.\n\n  # VXLAN decoder is assigned to up to 4 UDP ports. By default only the\n  # IANA assigned port 4789 is enabled.\n  vxlan:\n    enabled: true\n    ports: $VXLAN_PORTS # syntax: '[8472, 4789]' or '4789'.\n\n  # Geneve decoder is assigned to up to 4 UDP ports. By default only the\n  # IANA assigned port 6081 is enabled.\n  geneve:\n    enabled: true\n    ports: $GENEVE_PORTS # syntax: '[6081, 1234]' or '6081'.\n\n  # maximum number of decoder layers for a packet\n  # max-layers: 16\n\n##\n## Performance tuning and profiling\n##\n\n# The detection engine builds internal groups of signatures. The engine\n# allows us to specify the profile to use for them, to manage memory in an\n# efficient way keeping good performance. For the profile keyword you\n# can use the words \"low\", \"medium\", \"high\" or \"custom\". If you use custom,\n# make sure to define the values in the \"custom-values\" section.\n# Usually you would prefer medium/high/low.\n#\n# \"sgh mpm-context\", indicates how the staging should allot mpm contexts for\n# the signature groups.  \"single\" indicates the use of a single context for\n# all the signature group heads.  \"full\" indicates a mpm-context for each\n# group head.  \"auto\" lets the engine decide the distribution of contexts\n# based on the information the engine gathers on the patterns from each\n# group head.\n#\n# The option inspection-recursion-limit is used to limit the recursive calls\n# in the content inspection code.  For certain payload-sig combinations, we\n# might end up taking too much time in the content inspection code.\n# If the argument specified is 0, there are no limits on the recursion.\n# When a value is not specified, the default is 3000\ndetect:\n  profile: medium\n  custom-values:\n    toclient-groups: 3\n    toserver-groups: 25\n  sgh-mpm-context: auto\n  #inspection-recursion-limit: 3000\n  # try to tie an app-layer transaction for rules without app-layer keywords\n  # if there is only one live transaction for the flow\n  # allows to log app-layer metadata in alert\n  # but the transaction may not be the relevant one.\n  # guess-applayer-tx: no\n  # If set to yes, the loading of signatures will be made after the capture\n  # is started. This will limit the downtime in IPS mode.\n  #delayed-detect: yes\n\n  prefilter:\n    # default prefiltering setting. \"mpm\" only creates MPM/fast_pattern\n    # engines. \"auto\" also sets up prefilter engines for other keywords.\n    # Use --list-keywords=all to see which keywords support prefiltering.\n    default: mpm\n\n  # the grouping values above control how many groups are created per\n  # direction. Port whitelisting forces that port to get its own group.\n  # Very common ports will benefit, as well as ports with many expensive\n  # rules.\n  grouping:\n    #tcp-whitelist: 53, 80, 139, 443, 445, 1433, 3306, 3389, 6666, 6667, 8080\n    #udp-whitelist: 53, 135, 5060\n\n  profiling:\n    # Log the rules that made it past the prefilter stage, per packet\n    # default is off. The threshold setting determines how many rules\n    # must have made it past pre-filter for that rule to trigger the\n    # logging.\n    #inspect-logging-threshold: 200\n    grouping:\n      dump-to-disk: false\n      include-rules: false      # very verbose\n      include-mpm-stats: false\n\n# Select the multi pattern algorithm you want to run for scan/search the\n# in the engine.\n#\n# The supported algorithms are:\n# \"ac\"      - Aho-Corasick, default implementation\n# \"ac-bs\"   - Aho-Corasick, reduced memory implementation\n# \"ac-ks\"   - Aho-Corasick, \"Ken Steele\" variant\n# \"hs\"      - Hyperscan, available when built with Hyperscan support\n#\n# The default mpm-algo value of \"auto\" will use \"hs\" if Hyperscan is\n# available, \"ac\" otherwise.\n#\n# The mpm you choose also decides the distribution of mpm contexts for\n# signature groups, specified by the conf - \"detect.sgh-mpm-context\".\n# Selecting \"ac\" as the mpm would require \"detect.sgh-mpm-context\"\n# to be set to \"single\", because of ac's memory requirements, unless the\n# ruleset is small enough to fit in memory, in which case one can\n# use \"full\" with \"ac\".  The rest of the mpms can be run in \"full\" mode.\n\nmpm-algo: auto\n\n# Select the matching algorithm you want to use for single-pattern searches.\n#\n# Supported algorithms are \"bm\" (Boyer-Moore) and \"hs\" (Hyperscan, only\n# available if Suricata has been built with Hyperscan support).\n#\n# The default of \"auto\" will use \"hs\" if available, otherwise \"bm\".\n\nspm-algo: auto\n\n# Suricata is multi-threaded. Here the threading can be influenced.\nthreading:\n  set-cpu-affinity: no\n  # Tune cpu affinity of threads. Each family of threads can be bound\n  # to specific CPUs.\n  #\n  # These 2 apply to the all runmodes:\n  # management-cpu-set is used for flow timeout handling, counters\n  # worker-cpu-set is used for 'worker' threads\n  #\n  # Additionally, for autofp these apply:\n  # receive-cpu-set is used for capture threads\n  # verdict-cpu-set is used for IPS verdict threads\n  #\n  cpu-affinity:\n    - management-cpu-set:\n        cpu: [ 0 ]  # include only these CPUs in affinity settings\n    - receive-cpu-set:\n        cpu: [ 0 ]  # include only these CPUs in affinity settings\n    - worker-cpu-set:\n        cpu: [ \"all\" ]\n        mode: \"exclusive\"\n        # Use explicitly 3 threads and don't compute number by using\n        # detect-thread-ratio variable:\n        # threads: 3\n        prio:\n          low: [ 0 ]\n          medium: [ \"1-2\" ]\n          high: [ 3 ]\n          default: \"medium\"\n    #- verdict-cpu-set:\n    #    cpu: [ 0 ]\n    #    prio:\n    #      default: \"high\"\n  #\n  # By default Suricata creates one \"detect\" thread per available CPU/CPU core.\n  # This setting allows controlling this behaviour. A ratio setting of 2 will\n  # create 2 detect threads for each CPU/CPU core. So for a dual core CPU this\n  # will result in 4 detect threads. If values below 1 are used, less threads\n  # are created. So on a dual core CPU a setting of 0.5 results in 1 detect\n  # thread being created. Regardless of the setting at a minimum 1 detect\n  # thread will always be created.\n  #\n  detect-thread-ratio: 1.0\n  #\n  # By default, the per-thread stack size is left to its default setting. If\n  # the default thread stack size is too small, use the following configuration\n  # setting to change the size. Note that if any thread's stack size cannot be\n  # set to this value, a fatal error occurs.\n  #\n  # Generally, the per-thread stack-size should not exceed 8MB.\n  #stack-size: 8mb\n\n# Luajit has a strange memory requirement, its 'states' need to be in the\n# first 2G of the process' memory.\n#\n# 'luajit.states' is used to control how many states are preallocated.\n# State use: per detect script: 1 per detect thread. Per output script: 1 per\n# script.\nluajit:\n  states: 128\n\n# Profiling settings. Only effective if Suricata has been built with\n# the --enable-profiling configure flag.\n#\nprofiling:\n  # Run profiling for every X-th packet. The default is 1, which means we\n  # profile every packet. If set to 1024, one packet is profiled for every\n  # 1024 received. The sample rate must be a power of 2.\n  #sample-rate: 1024\n\n  # rule profiling\n  rules:\n\n    # Profiling can be disabled here, but it will still have a\n    # performance impact if compiled in.\n    enabled: yes\n    filename: rule_perf.log\n    append: yes\n    # Set active to yes to enable rules profiling at start\n    # if set to no (default), the rules profiling will have to be started\n    # via unix socket commands.\n    #active:no\n\n    # Sort options: ticks, avgticks, checks, matches, maxticks\n    # If commented out all the sort options will be used.\n    #sort: avgticks\n\n    # Limit the number of sids for which stats are shown at exit (per sort).\n    limit: 10\n\n    # output to json\n    json: yes\n\n  # per keyword profiling\n  keywords:\n    enabled: yes\n    filename: keyword_perf.log\n    append: yes\n\n  prefilter:\n    enabled: yes\n    filename: prefilter_perf.log\n    append: yes\n\n  # per rulegroup profiling\n  rulegroups:\n    enabled: yes\n    filename: rule_group_perf.log\n    append: yes\n\n  # packet profiling\n  packets:\n\n    # Profiling can be disabled here, but it will still have a\n    # performance impact if compiled in.\n    enabled: yes\n    filename: packet_stats.log\n    append: yes\n\n    # per packet csv output\n    csv:\n\n      # Output can be disabled here, but it will still have a\n      # performance impact if compiled in.\n      enabled: no\n      filename: packet_stats.csv\n\n  # profiling of locking. To activate\n# this mode, you need to set mode to 'repeat'\n# If you want a packet to be sent to another queue after an ACCEPT decision\n# set the mode to 'route' and set next-queue value.\n# On Linux >= 3.1, you can set batchcount to a value > 1 to improve performance\n# by processing several packets before sending a verdict (worker runmode only).\n# On Linux >= 3.6, you can set the fail-open option to yes to have the kernel\n# accept the packet if Suricata is not able to keep pace.\n# bypass mark and mask can be used to implement NFQ bypass. If bypass mark is\n# set then the NFQ bypass is activated. Suricata will set the bypass mark/mask\n# on packet of a flow that need to be bypassed. The Netfilter ruleset has to\n# directly accept all packets of a flow once a packet has been marked.\nnfq:\n#  mode: accept\n#  repeat-mark: 1\n#  repeat-mask: 1\n#  bypass-mark: 1\n#  bypass-mask: 1\n#  route-queue: 2\n#  batchcount: 20\n#  fail-open: yes\n\n#nflog support\nnflog:\n    # netlink multicast group\n    # (the same as the iptables --nflog-group param)\n    # Group 0 is used by the kernel, so you can't use it\n  - group: 2\n    # netlink buffer size\n    buffer-size: 18432\n    # put default value here\n  - group: default\n    # set number of packets to queue inside kernel\n    qthreshold: 1\n    # set the delay before flushing packet in the kernel's queue\n    qtimeout: 100\n    # netlink max buffer size\n    max-size: 20000\n\n##\n## Advanced Capture Options\n##\n\n# General settings affecting packet capture\ncapture:\n  # disable NIC offloading. It's restored when Suricata exits.\n  # Enabled by default.\n  #disable-offloading: false\n  #\n  # disable checksum validation. Same as setting '-k none' on the\n  # command-line.\n  #checksum-validation: none\n\n# Netmap support\n#\n# Netmap operates with NIC directly in driver, so you need FreeBSD 11+ which has\n# built-in Netmap support or compile and install the Netmap module and appropriate\n# NIC driver for your Linux system.\n# To reach maximum throughput disable all receive-, segmentation-,\n# checksum- offloading on your NIC (using ethtool or similar).\n# Disabling TX checksum offloading is *required* for connecting OS endpoint\n# with NIC endpoint.\n# You can find more information at https://github.com/luigirizzo/netmap\n#\nnetmap:\n   # To specify OS endpoint add plus sign at the end (e.g. \"eth0+\")\n - interface: eth2\n   # Number of capture threads. \"auto\" uses number of RSS queues on interface.\n   # Warning: unless the RSS hashing is symmetrical, this will lead to\n   # accuracy issues.\n   #threads: auto\n   # You can use the following variables to activate netmap tap or IPS mode.\n   # If copy-mode is set to ips or tap, the traffic coming to the current\n   # interface will be copied to the copy-iface interface. If 'tap' is set, the\n   # copy is complete. If 'ips' is set, the packet matching a 'drop' action\n   # will not be copied.\n   # To specify the OS as the copy-iface (so the OS can route packets, or forward\n   # to a service running on the same machine) add a plus sign at the end\n   # (e.g. \"copy-iface: eth0+\"). Don't forget to set up a symmetrical eth0+ -> eth0\n   # for return packets. The pcap filter syntax apply here.\n   #bpf-filter: port 80 or udp\n #- interface: eth3\n   #threads: auto\n   #copy-mode: tap\n   #copy-iface: eth2\n   # Put default values here\n - interface: default\n\n# PF_RING configuration: for use with native PF_RING support\n# for more info see http://www.ntop.org/products/pf_ring/\npfring:\n  - interface: eth0\n    # Number of receive threads. If set to 'auto' Suricata will first try\n    # to use CPU (core) count and otherwise RSS queue count.\n    threads: auto\n\n    # Default clusterid.  PF_RING will load balance packets based on flow.\n    # All threads/processes that will participate need to have the same\n    # clusterid.\n    cluster-id: 99\n\n    # Default PF_RING cluster type. For Example:\n#\n#   ipfw add 100 divert 8000 ip from any to any\n#\n# N.B. This example uses \"8000\" -- this number must mach the values\n# you passed on the command line, i.e., -d 8000\n#\nipfw:\n\n  # Reinject packets at the specified ipfw rule number.  This config\n  # option is the ipfw rule number AT WHICH rule processing continues\n  # in the ipfw processing system after the engine has finished\n  # inspecting the packet for acceptance.  If no rule number is specified,\n  # accepted packets are reinjected at the divert rule which they entered\n  # and IPFW rule processing continues.  No check is done to verify\n  # this will rule makes sense so care must be taken to avoid loops in ipfw.\n  #\n  ## The following example tells the engine to reinject packets\n  # back into the ipfw firewall AT rule number 5500:\n  #\n  # ipfw-reinjection-rule-number: 5500\n\n\nnapatech:\n    # When use_all_streams is set to \"yes\" the initialization code will query\n    # the Napatech service for all configured streams and listen on all of them.\n    # When set to \"no\" the streams config array will be used.\n    #\n    # This option necessitates running the appropriate NTPL commands to create\n    # the desired streams prior to running Suricata.\n    #use-all-streams: no\n\n    # The streams to listen on when auto-config is disabled or when and threading\n    # cpu-affinity is disabled.  This can be either:\n    #   an individual stream (e.g. streams: [0])\n    # or\n    #   a range of streams (e.g. streams: [\"0-3\"])\n    #\n    streams: [\"0-3\"]\n\n    # Stream stats can be enabled to provide fine grain packet and byte counters\n    # for each thread/stream that is configured.\n    #\n    enable-stream-stats: no\n\n    # When auto-config is enabled the streams will be created and assigned\n    # automatically to the NUMA node where the thread resides.  If cpu-affinity\n    # is enabled in the threading section.  Then the streams will be created\n    # according to the number of worker threads specified in the worker-cpu-set.\n    # Otherwise, the streams array is used to define the streams.\n    #\n    # This option is intended primarily to support legacy configurations.\n    #\n    # This option cannot be used simultaneously with either \"use-all-streams\"\n    # or \"hardware-bypass\".\n    #\n    auto-config: yes\n\n    # Enable hardware level flow bypass.\n    #\n    hardware-bypass: yes\n\n    # Enable inline operation.  When enabled traffic arriving on a given port is\n    # automatically forwarded out its peer port after analysis by Suricata.\n    #\n    inline: no\n\n    # Ports indicates which Napatech ports are to be used in auto-config mode.\n    # these are the port IDs of the ports that will be merged prior to the\n    # traffic being distributed to the streams.\n    #\n    # When hardware-bypass is enabled the ports must be configured as a segment.\n    # specify the port(s) on which upstream and downstream traffic will arrive.\n    # This information is necessary for the hardware to properly process flows.\n    #\n    # When using a tap configuration one of the ports will receive inbound traffic\n    # for the network and the other will receive outbound traffic.
